# Assumption and proxy registry

Every `PROXY` and every `[ASSUMPTION]` in code or in the dictionary appears here (RULES.md rule 2; architecture §15).
An unlabelled proxy is a defect.

**Columns:**

| Column | Content |
|---|---|
| ID | `A-<WP>-<n>` |
| Statement | What is assumed or approximated |
| Tag | `[ASSUMPTION]` or `PROXY` |
| Source / evidence | Where the value comes from (report § or note file, or "none public") |
| Sweep range | Range explored when the value isn't public |
| Affects | Modules and tests |
| Exposing test | The test that would reveal the limitation |
| Replacement route | How it gets replaced (data request, calibration, …) |
| Status | open / replaced / retired |

Each WP appends rows only inside its own section.

| A-W0.3-9 | **R-ICD-WORDS** AS15532-style data words and per-message validity words. Analog quantities are 16-bit two's-complement BNR words with LSB = recorder LSB; quantities that do not fit use 2 words, most-significant word first; discretes are bit-packed; each message starts with one validity word per 16 data items (bit set = BUS_INVALID, AD-45), in the style of the F/A-18C ADC validity words; | A-W0.3-19 | **R-LSB-BNR** Power-of-two BNR LSB computed from the full-scale range. Where the inventory gives no numeric quantisation (for example '16-bit BNR; choose LSB so steady-state sigma is ~0.3-1.5 LSB'), the LSB is the smallest power of two 2^k with which the full-scale range [rmin, rmax] fits an n-bit AS15532-style word (icd.bnr_lsb). Two's complement when rmin < 0: counts -2^(n-1) .. 2^(n-1) - 1, so LSB >= max(-rmin / 2^(n-1), max(rmax, 0) / (2^(n-1) - 1)) (the negative side holds one more count). Unsigned when rmin >= 0: counts 0 .. 2^n - 1, so LSB >= rmax / (2^n - 1). n = 16 for one 1553 word, 32 for a two-word (MSW-first) quantity, 12 for an FTI PCM word; on ARINC 429 the 19-bit BNR field (18 data bits + sign) gives LSB >= max(abs(rmin), abs(rmax)) / (2^18 - 1). The smallest word that fits the range at that LSB is then chosen. A value that leaves the aircraft only in a download or maintenance record (float64, no recorder word) keeps the LSB of the row's 16-bit word as its native (acquisition) step and has no recorder LSB. | [ASSUMPTION] | parameter_inventory.csv recommended_quantization; data_pathway_and_formats.md Q2 Inferences; src/phalanx/dictionary/icd.py bnr_lsb | LSB x0.25-x4; for two-word (32-bit) quantities from the 32-bit LSB up to the 16-bit (MSW-only) LSB, x1-x65536; re-choose so steady-state sigma/LSB is 0.3-1.5 once M13 noise is calibrated | M13, M15; 24 params: AD: BOOM; BRK: PEDAL, PRESS, WHEEL_SPEED; ECS: AVIONICS_T, BLEED_P, BLEED_T, CABIN_P, COCKPIT_T, TCV; ELEC: BACKUP.STATUS, BATT; FUEL: TANK_PRESS, TEMP; LSS: LOX_QTY, OBOGS; NAV: INS.ALT, INS.LAT, INS.LON; NWS: STATE; SPS: JFS.EGT, JFS.OIL_QTY, JFS.SPEED; STRUCT: STRAIN | tests/property/test_dictionary_properties.py::test_bnr_lsb_fits_and_is_minimal | Tejas ICD scaling | open |

| A-W0.3-37 | **R-CODES-SYNTHETIC** Synthetic code tables. SSS-NNN BIT/fault codes, CWS legend slots, FCS law, GPS fix / figure of merit / INS mode, borescope grade and store-station codes are synthetic Phalanx tables (AD-19); real Tejas code structures, the CWS legend list and the IAF forms are not public. The GPS figure of merit is a 9-level code (the inventory gives its units as 'enum' and its quantisation as 'bits'); the number of levels and their order are synthetic. 0.1.2: FCS law 3 = GROUND (W1.2), fighter flight phases 8-15 (W1.1), and one unified BIT/fault/exceedance table `bit_codes` (104 codes: M14's 102 plus two M08 afterburner faults; severities, fault lists, CWS legend and voice mapping, monitor limit ids; AD-48 item 5) with a documented map from M08's earlier codes. | [ASSUMPTION] | architecture AD-19; tejas_avionics_and_parameter_inventory.md Q3/Q6; parameter_inventory.csv notes ('Tejas code structure not public', 'Legend list not public') | none (labels only) | M02, M05, M08, M14, M17, M18; 8 params: CWS: LEGEND; ENG: FAULT_CODES; FCS: BIT.LOG, MODE; MX: BORESCOPE; NAV: GPS.STATUS; STORES: CONFIG; SYS: PHASE | tests/unit/test_dictionary_compile.py::test_code_tables_marked_synthetic | CWS legend list and BIT code structure (architecture §19 request) | open |

| A-W0.3-40 | **R-RANGE-FUEL-TOTAL** Total-fuel full scale from internal plus the largest external fuel load. FUEL.TOTAL full scale = 0 to internal fuel 2,458 kg (the inventory range) plus the largest stated drop-tank load, three tanks of 2 x 1,200 L + 800 L = 3,200 L (a three-drop-tank ferry is demonstrated), at the internal-fuel density 2,458 kg / 3,060 L = 0.803 kg/L (about 2,570 kg): about 5,030 kg, rounded up to 6,000 kg. The capacities are DIRECT (secondary); that one total word covers external fuel, and the rounding margin, are assumed. | [ASSUMPTION] | parameter_inventory.csv FUEL.TOTAL range ('0-2,458 internal (+ drop tanks 1,200/800/725 L)'); tejas_utility_systems.md KQ3 (2,458 kg / 3,060 L; three-drop-tank ferry) (basis: internal 2,458 kg / 3,060 L and 1,200/800/725 L drop tanks [DIRECT, secondary]) | full scale 5,030-8,000 kg (LSB 1 kg from the inventory; a 16-bit unsigned word holds up to 65,535 kg) | M09, M13, M15; 1 params: FUEL: TOTAL | tests/unit/test_dictionary_validate.py::test_range_lsb_word_consistency | ECFM-C gauging specification | open |
| A-W0.3-41 | **R-FADEC-ADC** FADEC lane converter step from an assumed 14-bit FADEC converter. FADEC lane channels (ENG.*.A/B, role lane, digitised inside the FADEC) carry the FADEC's own converter resolution in converter_step = full-scale span / 2^14 (analog lanes adc_bits = 14; speed lanes the equivalent 14-bit counter resolution). The IN20 FADEC bit resolution is not public (f404_in20_engine_model.md Gaps; empirical_dashlink_texture.md: 'F404-IN20 FADEC/1553 word resolution is unknown'); across the whole sweep the converter step stays finer than the recorded word LSB (PS3 0.5 psi, the -400 MSDC resolution), which is what the FADEC control loop needs (W1.3b: 0.5 psi PS3 steps make fuel flicker). Since 0.1.3 this step is the lanes' native_step (until 0.1.2 it was carried as converter_step while M13 still scaled its MEASURED lane noise by the native step; M13 now quantises the lanes at this step with the lane noise in EU, W2.2). | [ASSUMPTION] | f404_in20_engine_model.md (Gaps: IN20 FADEC bit resolution not public; MSDC resolutions PS3 0.5 psia, N 16 rpm, T 1 K); empirical_dashlink_texture.md Q3; W1.3b report; src/phalanx/acquisition/config.py (sigma = sigma_steps x native_step) | 12-16 bits (converter step span/4096 .. span/65536) | M08, M12, M13; 6 params: ENG: A8, N1, N2, PLA, PS3, T1 | tests/unit/test_dictionary_validate.py::test_fadec_lanes_carry_the_fadec_converter_step | IN20 FADEC converter specification | open |
| A-W0.3-42 | **R-AD-PORT-PRESSURE** AD.DPA/DPB are flow-angle port pressures (absolute), not differential pressures. The inventory names AD.DPA/DPB 'differential pressure' but gives 35-1500 mbar, the range Kala (2015) quotes for the ADC transducers on the flow-angle ports (P-alpha1/beta1, P-alpha2/beta2; 'four pressure transducers fitted inside each ADC ... 35 to 1300 mbar (Ps); 35-2700 mbar (Pt), 35-1500 mbar (P-alpha/beta)'). A range starting at 35 mbar, like the absolute Ps/Pt transducers, is an absolute range, so the channels are absolute port pressures from which the ADC computes AoA/AoSS by differencing; the range is kept as written (DIRECT) and the pressure reference is absolute. | [ASSUMPTION] | parameter_inventory.csv AD.DPA/AD.DPB; tejas_airframe_aero_fcs.md (Kala 2015 transducer quote); report §4 air data (basis: transducer range 35-1500 mbar [DIRECT, Kala (ADE) JOAST 67(2A) 2015 p. 215]) | pressure reference absolute (35-1500 mbar) or differential (signed, +/-1465 mbar span) | M12, M13; 2 params: AD: DPA, DPB | tests/unit/test_dictionary_validate.py::test_e0b_ad_port_pressures | ADC interface specification | open |
| A-W0.3-43 | **R-CODE-WORD** Code-word bit layout of SSS-NNN fault-code channels. A code_word channel carries one synthetic SSS-NNN code per 16-bit word: prefix index k (position of the prefix in the channel's prefixes list) in bits 15-10 and NNN in bits 9-0; the EU value (CIR and contract) is k * 1000 + NNN, and 0 means no active code. Moved into the dictionary (bit_code_format.word_layout) from the W1.7 bus encoder (A-W1.7-6). | [ASSUMPTION] | src/phalanx/bus/words.py (W1.7, A-W1.7-6); R-CODES-SYNTHETIC defines the format, not the bit layout | any injective layout (no physical effect) | M08, M14, M15, M17; 2 params: ENG: FAULT_CODES; FCS: BIT.LOG | tests/unit/test_dictionary_codes.py::test_code_word_layout_round_trips_every_code | Tejas ICD fault-word format | open |
| A-W0.3-44 | **R-REC-SETUP** Recorder setup - recording events and time-based index. The SYNTHETIC recorder (R-1) records event and index packets: three recorder events (REC_START, TIME_LOCK_LOST, TIME_LOCK_REGAINED; type R, priority 1, no initial capture, limit count 65,535) and a time-based index every 10 s, as M16 writes them (IRIG 106-15 10.6.7.3: index packets are enabled when event packets are; TMATS Table 9-3 R-x\EV, R-x\IDX). The TMATS generator emits these attributes from the dictionary. | [ASSUMPTION] | src/phalanx/recorder/setup.py and writer.py (W1.7, A-W1.7-12); research_sources/data_pathway/ch9_trmc_113019518.txt (basis: event/index semantics TRANSFER: IRIG 106-15 Ch10 10.6.7 and Ch9 Table 9-3) | index interval 1-60 s (no Tejas recorder setup is public) | M16, M17; 0 params:  | tests/unit/test_dictionary_tmats.py::test_recording_attributes_equal_the_m16_overlay | Tejas recorder setup (not public) | open |

Not public. | [ASSUMPTION] | configs/tejas/params/M09.yaml jfs.jfs_rated_speed (A-W1.4-26: 100 kW-class centrifugal gas generators run ~50-70 krpm) | 40,000-80,000 rpm | M09, M13; 0 params: ; constants: channel_reference.jfs_rated_speed | tests/unit/test_dictionary_eu.py::test_jfs_reference_equals_the_m09_parameter | GTSU-110 data sheet | open |
| A-W0.3-46 | **R-AD51-COMPUTED** The radar-altimeter height is an LRU-computed digital output (AD-51), not an analog reading. The inventory classes NAV.RADALT as measured. The radar altimeter's height word is the output of the LRU's own range tracker (internally digitised, acquisition LRU-INT), and AD-51 makes it an output of the M12 avionics extension supplied to the recorder as a RECORDED (LRU-computed) value, like the INS and AHRS outputs. The channel's signal class is therefore computed (no recorder-side converter, no adc_bits); range, LSB, bus and sinks are unchanged. | [ASSUMPTION] | docs/architecture.md AD-51; parameter_inventory.csv NAV.RADALT (rate_group_acquisition LRU-INT) (basis: AD-51 (architecture decision register); the radar-altimeter tracker model in phalanx.sensors.avionics.radalt) | none (classification) | M12, M13, M15; 1 params: NAV: RADALT | tests/integration/test_w22_avionics_ports.py::test_recorded_channels_against_the_dictionary | Tejas radio-altimeter ICD (output word definition) | open |

## W0.4 Contract, CIR, storage, replay, fast path

These are interface choices (no physical value is assumed), so "sweep range" lists the alternatives held open.
The authoritative text of each statement is in `src/phalanx/dictionary/contract/spec/v0/contract.yaml`
(`assumptions:`); the generated summary lists them for owner review at E0.

| A-W0.5-4 | **Decided (owner, PD-W0.5-2 option a; AD-40): G0 is one-sided by default** — it FAILs only if the lower bound of the 95 % CI of the AUC is above 0.5; an AUC below chance never FAILs. Evidence: the pre-registered **two-sided** rule (CI must include 0.5) falsely FAILed 6/20 null replicates (independent draws from one distribution, 40 pairs, 500 resamples), every time with the CI entirely **below** 0.5 (grouped-CV anti-learning), never above; under the one-sided rule none of the 20 would FAIL (largest CI lower bound 0.484). The two-sided rule stays available as `sided="two"`, used only to reproduce round 2. | A-W1.3a-9 | **PROXY (structural limit, AD-46)**: surge as an event. When min(fan, HPC) margin (constant speed, map stall line) falls below sm_trigger, fan and HPC delivery collapse to surge_c_min (0.12, derived from the HARV CDP ratio) and recover quadratically within 0.2 s, efficiency x0.5 at the bottom; no post-stall (rotating stall / deep surge) dynamics. Resulting CDP collapse about 0.26 of pre-surge vs 0.12 (HARV). | A-W1.3b-1 | The FADEC runs on its own clock domain, 60 Hz nominal, sharing M13's FADEC acquisition oscillator (`ports.DEFAULT_CLOCK = acquisition_clock("FADEC", 60)`: +50 ppm, 3.1 ms phase, A-W1.6-21; AD-42); at truth step k it processes the frames whose instants fall in [t_{k-1}, t_k) and its commands apply from t_k. Software uses the nominal frame period (1/f_nom) for its integrators and timers | [ASSUMPTION] | AD-39, AD-42, A-W0.3-25 (60 Hz in the 50-67 Hz band); architecture §8 (clock-domain update applied at the first truth frame after the compute instant, A-W0.2-3) | f_nom 50-67 Hz; offset/phase per M13 clock_fadec sweep | M08, M12/M13 in-loop lane source, M15, W2.1 wiring | `tests/integration/test_fadec_coupling.py::test_lane_source_frames_equal_the_fadec_frame_schedule`, `::test_scheduler_wiring_reproduces_the_fused_loop_bitwise`; `tests/integration/test_fadec_m13_lanes.py::test_m13_sample_counts_are_the_fadec_frame_schedule` | IN20 FADEC frame rate and oscillator tolerance (GE/HAL) | open |
| A-W1.3b-2 | Core fuel law in **ratio units**: the N2 governor and every limiter output Wf/PS3, MIN/MAX selection is in ratio units, and Wf_cmd = RU_sel x (0.05 s filtered) validated PS3. Direct-Wf law (C-MAPSS40k style) kept as `fuel_law = 0`. **Mechanism of the A2 sign (AD-50):** the negative small-signal FF->N2 lag comes from the ratio-unit PS3-proportional metering with WEAK small-signal governing, not from speed-error governing: it survives governor gains of zero, Kp >= 3-5 destroys it (FF becomes anti-correlated), and the direct-Wf law is anti-correlated (its positive peak 'lag' is the argmax of a side-lobe). On the fixture the 4 Hz B2 of -0.25 s is the FF meter's (0.00 s with an ideal meter; truth at 20 Hz -0.05..-0.15 s). Magnitude (-0.50 s real) is identified jointly with ps3_tau, gains and the disturbance mix in W2.4a, preserving fast-band r(N2,FF) ~ +0.67 | [ASSUMPTION] | report §3 (accel/decel on Wf/PS3; AB in Wr/PS3 ratio units); f404 notes Q4 (MFC PS3 input); round-2 REPORT §3.2, AD-31 as refined by AD-50 (W1.3b independent review on fixture and real M07) | fuel_law in {0, 1} | M08 small-signal FF->N2 lead-lag (B2) and fast-band FF correlations (B1), M07 coupling | `tests/integration/test_fadec_closed_loop.py::test_fast_band_ff_correlations_are_positive_under_process_disturbance`, `::test_negative_lag_survives_zero_governor_gains`, `::test_direct_wf_law_is_anti_correlated`; `tests/statistical/test_fadec_leadlag.py` | W2.4a closed-loop identification on DASHlink proxy (direct vs indirect, round-2 REPORT §8 H-c 2) | open |

| A-W1.7-1 | Each LRU unit has one crystal: frame instants `floor(phi + n*1e9/(f(1+Delta)))`; sub-rates (producer groups, ARINC transmit cycles) are phase-locked to the same crystal. For the LRUs M13 acquires on (FADEC, DFCC, ADC, BHEEM_EU, ECFM_C, FTI_DAS) the crystal is M13's acquisition clock (A-W1.6-21, fixed offsets/phases; `bus.clocks.acquisition_oscillators`, passed as `oscillators=`); for the other LRUs (OAC, INGPS, RADAR, SMS, ...) and stand-alone tests Delta ~ N(0, 20 ppm) per unit serial (constant across sorties), phi uniform per sortie (keyed draws) | [ASSUMPTION] | Arch §8 (per-LRU oscillator); report §13 ("crystal tolerance of tens of ppm"); A-W0.2-1 (constant offset per run) | sigma 1-100 ppm (`M15.yaml clocks.lru_offset_sigma`) | M15, M16 (slip statistics), M13 (shares the oscillator, W2.2) | `tests/statistical/test_bus_clock_stats.py` (slip period 1/(f*dDelta), dup/skip rates), `tests/integration/test_bus_m13_clocks.py` (M13 instants on the M15 crystal) | Real LRU crystal specs or measured bus traces | open |
| A-W1.7-2 | Acquisition-to-transmit-buffer latency 8 ms for every RT LRU (data acquired at a tick are written 8 ms later) | [ASSUMPTION] | Tejas DFCC computation delay 8 ms (Patel et al., JOAST 58(4) 2006, via tejas_airframe_aero_fcs.md Q3), extrapolated to all LRUs | 1-25 ms | M15 (t_bus - t_sample), M17 (nominal latency) | `tests/integration/test_bus_encoder.py::test_latency_and_age_follow_the_clock_model` | Tejas ICD data-latency table | open |

| A-W1.7-4 | BC schedule: minor frame = one OAC tick (50 Hz); sub-rate messages on the least-loaded phase (greedy, ties to the smallest phase); messages back to back, fastest group first, 28 us gaps, first command at the tick (+0 us) | [ASSUMPTION] | Alta/Bloomy BC scheduling practice (data_pathway_and_formats.md Q2); KC-135 sample gaps (TRANSFER) | frame start 0-1000 us; fixed-slot vs back-to-back schedule | M15 | `tests/unit/test_bus_schedule.py` (loading <= 50 %, every message at its rate) | Tejas BC schedule | open |

| A-W1.7-18 | **Conflict log (E1-HY): clock-offset magnitude.** Four statements disagree: (1) report §8 (R:563) replaces the "tens of ppm" oscillator assumption by "a per-LRU offset parameter whose measured proxy value is ≈910 ppm"; (2) report §13 (R:1036) reads the same 910 ppm as "much larger than a crystal's tolerance of tens of ppm", i.e. a nominal-rate mismatch rather than oscillator error [MEASURED; interpretation ASSUMPTION]; (3) architecture §8 quotes Δ ≈ 910 ppm as the measured DASHlink recorder-vs-source offset (AD-06), without saying whether it is a crystal or a rate property; (4) M15 `lru_offset_sigma` and M16 `rtc_offset_sigma` use σ = 20 ppm. **Which value applies where, and why:** σ = 20 ppm (sweep 1–100) is the per-unit *crystal* error of LRU oscillators (M15) and the recorder RTC (M16), following R:1036 (crystal tolerance tens of ppm); a nominal-rate mismatch is not a crystal property, and M15 already represents nominal-rate differences explicitly through the dictionary rate groups (a 40 Hz producer polled at 50 Hz), so adding 910 ppm to the crystal σ would double-count it. Δ_conv = 910 ppm (sweep 20–1,000) is used only as M13's converter-vs-read offset of the DASHlink-like analog acquisition (A-W1.6-24), where the slip mechanism depends on the beat between converter and read clock and DASHlink is the only measurement of that beat; its sweep covers both readings. The fixed M13 acquisition-oscillator offsets (e.g. FADEC +50 ppm, A-W1.6-21, sweep ±100 ppm) are crystal offsets within the same "tens of ppm" tolerance. Precedence (architecture > report) does not settle it, because architecture §8 only quotes the measurement; R:563 vs R:1036 is an internal report conflict, resolved here in R:1036's favour for crystals | [ASSUMPTION] (conflict resolution) | report §8 l.556–563 and §13 l.1036; architecture §8 and AD-06; round-1 `analysis/settling_experiment/results/mechanism_summary.json` P1 (Δ 910.5 ppm); E1 verifier §7 | crystal σ 1–100 ppm (M15, M16); Δ_conv 20–1,000 ppm (M13) | M13, M15, M16, M17 slip and time-base statistics | `tests/statistical/test_bus_clock_stats.py` (slip period 1/(f·ΔΔ)); `tests/unit/test_acquisition_slip.py::test_slip_period_is_one_over_f_delta` | Tejas LRU crystal specifications, or a Tejas bus/recorder trace showing slip periods; the coordinator may promote this resolution to the decision register | open |

| A-W2.1-8 | Take-off fix (coordinator assignment, W2.1a): (a) a stopped or slow braked main tyre holds up to the tyre's static friction `mu_brake_static` 0.8; the anti-skid limit `mu_brake_max` 0.40 applies above `v_antiskid` 5 m/s (linear blend in between). Root cause of the first run-up creep (~21 m/s in 30 s): M06 applied the rolling anti-skid limit to a stationary wheel (brake torque allows 2 x 35 kN, so torque is not the limit). (b) **run-up check at part power** (M02 `pla_runup` 73 deg = ~90 % N2, ~40 kN at SLS): with (a) the brakes hold T <= mu_s W (a/L) / (1 + mu_s h/L) ~ 0.54 W in M06 (the thrust's nose-down moment moves load to the unbraked nose wheel; a/L 0.885, h/L 0.39); measured: a MIL run-up on the ~10 t clean aircraft (gate sortie TJ213) broke away at the 54 kN thrust peak and rolled at 20 m/s. So the brakes do not hold MIL on a light aircraft, and the run-up runs to ~90 % N2, ~40 kN, ~75 % of the hold limit (the earlier W2.1a statement that MIL T/W ~0.41 stays below the hold limit used the drop-tank weight and ignored the load transfer; withdrawn). M02 sets the run-up power, so the plan and the flown PLA count the same usage cycles (the run-up is a CRUISE-level excursion, no longer a MAX one) | [TRANSFER: MIL-A-8866C] + [ASSUMPTION] | MIL-A-8866C §3.7.3 hard braking mu 0.8 (`flight_profiles_and_usage.md` D); the run-up power is not public for the Tejas: ~75 % of the M06 hold limit on the lightest configuration (physics, ASSUMPTION); ADA GS carbon brakes hold the aircraft against engine thrust (DIRECT, power not stated, `tejas_utility_systems.md`); anti-skid acts on wheel slip (ADA BMS); PLA -> N2 from the nominal M07/M08 trim (`fadec.trim.operating_point`) | mu_static 0.6-0.9; v_antiskid 2-10 m/s; pla_runup 65-80 deg | M06 brake force at low speed (run-up hold, taxi stops, last metres of the landing roll); M09 brake energy; M02 run-up segment power and the usage cycles it counts | `tests/integration/test_integration_takeoff.py::test_brakes_hold_the_runup_check` (N2 89.9-90.7 %, 40-42 kN, ground speed <= 0.2 m/s: the residual creep is M06's friction regularisation `v_eps` 0.2 m/s, A-W1.2-10) | Tejas / IAF run-up procedure and power (not public); ADA BMS / anti-skid low-speed cut-out data; tyre static friction data | open |
| A-W2.1-9 | Take-off fix (coordinator assignment, W2.1a): (a) **rotation**: on the main gear above `v_rotate` 80 m/s the pilot tracks a reference attitude rising at `q_rotate` 5 deg/s to `theta_rotate` 12 deg (aft stick = `k_rot_theta` x attitude error + `k_rot_q` x rate error, 0 to `stick_rotate` 0.8); M05's ground law links stick directly to elevon, so the former load-factor rotation command (3 g/rad -> 9 % stick -> -2.3 deg elevon) could not rotate the aircraft; airborne in the take-off the attitude goes through the load-factor law. (b) **physically gated segments**: the take-off ends when safely airborne (confirmed lift-off: all gear off for `t_liftoff` 1 s, climbing, `h_gear_up` 15 m above the field: the gear-up) and the landing roll starts at a confirmed touchdown (main gear on for `t_touchdown` 0.5 s), early or late; the pilot's plan clock shifts by the difference and later segments keep their planned durations; the gates apply to live transitions only (a boundary passed more than one tick ago, a plan loaded mid-flight, follows the plan's timing). M04 reports each segment change as `pilot.segment` and M02 (sortie mode with a segment source) sets its phase port and sends `ops.takeoff` / `ops.sortie_done` from them, so no phase follows the plan clock past a physical event (CLIMB cannot start on the runway). (c) **climb-out**: below `h_safe` 300 m above the departure field the energy guard keeps at least `gamma_min` 3 deg of climb (was a level-off at ~25 m), the climb-out stays wings level below `h_turn` 120 m and banks at most `bank_departure` 30 deg below `h_safe` (was a 60 deg departure turn at 20-30 m into the ground) | [ASSUMPTION] | rotation technique, rates, gear-up height and persistences not public for the Tejas (restricted-manual values are not used, report Appendix A1); h_turn: the civil instrument-departure criterion (ICAO Doc 8168 PANS-OPS, no turn before 120 m) as the reference value; persistences, gains and the IAF departure procedure not public | stick 0.5-1.0; q_rotate 3-8 deg/s; theta_rotate 8-14 deg; t_liftoff 0.5-3 s; t_touchdown 0.2-2 s; h_gear_up 5-30 m; gamma_min 1-5 deg; h_safe 150-600 m; h_turn 60-300 m; bank_departure 15-45 deg | M04 sticks and segment timing; M02 phase port and lifecycle events; M05 via the stick; everything timed on the sortie after the take-off | `tests/integration/test_integration_takeoff.py` (measured, TJ213 clean MIL: rotation at 81 m/s, lift-off at 90 m/s, pitch max 19.4 deg, alpha max 16.7 deg, 146 m climbed in the 30 s after the gear-up, largest height loss 1.7 m) | Tejas take-off technique, FBW ground-to-air transition and IAF departure procedure (not public) | open |
| A-W2.1-10 | Full-sortie fix (coordinator assignment, W2.1a, toward A6): a manoeuvre primitive pulls its target Nz for the first half of its dwell only if it is a pull-up (no turn, target Nz >= 2) and only while the flight-path angle is below `gamma_pull` (30 deg); the altitude law then levels the aircraft. Measured before the fix (gate sortie TJ212, ground attack): a turn primitive (pid 11, 2.6 g) that reached its heading fell into the pull-up rule and held 2.6 g wings level at MIL for up to 60 s: the aircraft looped from 3,000 m to a vertical climb, stalled at 5,800 m and 38 m/s and departed (alpha 60 deg, `loss_of_control` at 1,591 s). After that fix the same turn, on reaching its heading, gave way to the flight-path law towards the segment's 300 m AGL target: the bank-compensated law (nz = (cos gamma + correction) / cos phi) asked for full forward stick (-3.5 g) in a 69 deg bank; the aircraft bunted to -15.7 g and alpha -60 deg in 1.4 s (loss_of_control at 1,554 s). The airborne law now commands at least `nz_push_min` 0 g (path corrections unload, they do not push negative g). Then (2,155 s) a dive primitive (pid 10: PLA 34 deg, 4.5 g, -80 m/s, target 1,670 m lower) pulled its Nz at the start of the dwell from level flight at idle: the aircraft zoomed to 77 deg pitch and stalled at 53 m/s (loss_of_control at 2,169 s). A dive (target altitude below the previous segment's) now dives on the flight-path law and pulls its Nz as the pull-out, from the pull-out height v^2 (1 - cos gamma) / (g (Nz - 1)) above the target until level. Still open (TJ212, 2,193 s): a 4 g turn at MIL rolls out only within 0.15 rad of its heading; with the 0.6 1/s bank loop it overshoots by ~40 deg and reverses at 15 deg alpha / 150 m/s into a departure (loss_of_control at 2,200 s). A roll-out lead (turn rate over the bank-loop bandwidth) was tried and reverted: the 0 g push of the flight-path law at the branch switch then departed the aircraft at 2,007 s. The guidance law needs blended branch transitions (load-factor continuity) and energy management in sustained turns | [ASSUMPTION] | M02 primitive table (`configs/tejas/params/M02/primitives.csv`, A-W1.1-25); pull-up geometry and push limits not public | gamma_pull 15-60 deg; nz_push_min -1 to 0.5 g | M04 stick in pull-up primitives; the realised Nz and attitude spectra | A6 (`tests/integration/test_integration_smoke.py`, slow) | realised Nz / attitude spectra against the F-16 transfer (W2.6) | open |

A start failure (ops.start_failure, M11 target `ops`) before the take-off ground-aborts the sortie and closes M11/M14 with an aborted `ops.sortie_done` (operations mode, and telemetry with a segment source; the telemetry runner then ends the run as `SortieAborted('ground_abort')`); M05 takes its PBIT from `ops.power_up` (no `fcs.pbit_start`); the runner's `monitor.sortie_start` hook is gone | [DIRECT] anchor + [ASSUMPTION] | report Engine start row (-15 degC / 85 % battery / 18-42 h soak demonstration); configs/interfaces/w1_events.yaml ops.power_up (W2.5); AD-49; A-W2.5-2 | cold_soak_temperature 248-273 K | M05/M11/M14 power-up; M11 preflight modes; M02 ground aborts | `tests/integration/test_scenario_power_up.py`; `tests/integration/test_monitoring_power_up.py::test_m02_declares_the_power_up` | soak temperature from a ground-temperature history (overnight minimum) instead of the station temperature at the sortie | open |
| A-W2.1-12 | M04 guidance redesign (W2.1a-2, toward A6): (a) **one lateral law** for turns and track holding: track error -> turn-rate command (`k_track` 0.25 1/s) bounded by the turn rate of the segment's bank limit -> coordinated bank atan(V psi_dot / g); a turn primitive's bank limit is acos(1/n_turn) of its target Nz (a level turn at that bank pulls the target Nz through the bank-compensated flight-path law), otherwise `bank_max`; the roll-out begins as the error falls below psi_dot_max / k_track (no turn/track switch); (b) **energy management**: energy state e = 1 at or above the target speed, falling linearly to 0 at (1 - `speed_margin`) of it; a turn primitive's load factor relaxes to n_turn = 1 + (Nz_t - 1) e (30 deg bank floor), and a manoeuvre primitive trades height for speed with a descent of up to `gamma_trade` (1 - e) above `h_safe`; (c) the commanded load factor moves at most `nz_rate` 4 g/s (law changes ramp). Measured before: turn/track switches stepped the commanded Nz (5 g -> 0 g) and MIL turns bled 255 -> 148 m/s before departures at 2,062-2,200 s (TJ204, TJ212) | [ASSUMPTION] | research_notes/.../modelling_methodology.md §2 (deterministic guidance + autopilot loops, crossover-type pilot); sustained-turn energy balance (thrust = drag) as the physical basis; gains and rates not public | k_track 0.15-0.4 1/s; nz_rate 2-8 g/s; gamma_trade 0-10 deg | M04 sticks in all airborne segments; realised Nz, bank and energy histories | `tests/unit/test_pilot_guidance.py`; A6 (`tests/integration/test_integration_smoke.py`, slow) | realised turn, roll-out and energy histories against flight data (W2.6) | open |

| A-W2.1b-7 | **PROXY: no analytical redundancy in M08 signal validation.** The FADEC's model-based sensor cross-check (`vm_analytic`, on-board model vs validated lanes) stays OFF by owner decision (scope change, 2026-10-15): validation is range, rate and dual-lane cross-compare only. Missing: detection of an in-range sensor drift or a common-mode (both-lane) error by model comparison, and the model substitution that follows it. Effect: such sensor faults are never flagged by M08 (no ENG-04x/05x analytical codes, no substitution, no accommodation); the law runs on the wrong value, so the fault shows only through its physical consequences and through M14's own monitors - BIT coverage of drift-type sensor faults is understated against a real FADEC with analytical redundancy. The W2.1b M07-derived on-board model exists (steady line) but is not used: measured with the check on, no false failures at SLS/hot/30 kft, but a false T5 analytical fail on a 230 K day (harness span floor at low power, W2.3) | PROXY | owner scope decision (coordinator, 2026-10-15); AD-50; f404 notes Q4 (FICA-style on-board models in DEEC/FADEC practice) | `vm_analytic` 0/1 | M08 validation, M14/M11 detection coverage of sensor faults, BIT-event realism | `tests/unit/test_fadec_validator.py::test_failure_mode_common_mode_fault_defeats_voting` (a gross in-span common-mode T5 error passes with the check off); `tests/integration/test_fadec_obm.py` | Post-delivery: an M07-derived on-board model with transient terms, enabled after the W2.3 low-power T5 calibration | open |

After an **in-flight** power loss the INS/GPS solution stays frozen at its pre-loss values with its status words unchanged until the sortie ends: navigation invalid **without a flag**; on the ground it re-aligns (INS_MODE ALIGN, flagged) | [ASSUMPTION] | Report l.741 (electrical → avionics: partial electrical loss → navigation data invalid without a flag); faults_and_degradation.md (F-16 EGI: frozen or invalid data presented without a flag after a power loss, AIB 88-0549, DIRECT); MIL-STD-704F 16 V emergency floor and 50 ms transfer interrupts (tejas_utility_systems.md, TRANSFER); Tejas load allocation not public | v_dropout 12-18 V; hold-up 0.02-0.2 s; bus index 0-2 per LRU | M12 avionics, M14 (cross-checks), M15 (phase B RT silence) | `tests/unit/test_w22_avionics_power.py` | Tejas electrical load list / EGI power-interrupt behaviour from ADA | open |

## W2.5 Faults, BIT and the label-leak audit (M10, M11, M14; scope per AD-55)

| ID | Statement | Tag | Source / evidence | Sweep range | Affects | Exposing test | Replacement route | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A-W2.5-1 | Healthy-wear oil debris (C25): each engine serial has its own filter-debris rate, drawn once from a gamma distribution with Bennett's CF188 QFDA Fe fleet mean 0.4747 ug/h and 2 sigma 1.6813 ug/h (gamma form: positive and right-skewed, since 2 sigma > mean); the debris of a sortie is a gamma-process increment at that rate per engine-operating second with CV 0.3 over 100 EOT h (the CF filter/SOAP interval). Healthy wear adds to the filter debris only: the normal SOAP Fe slope (oil_fe_slope) is the oil-borne part of the same wear, so only spall debris raises oil Fe above it. The USN F/A-18 filter set (6.11 / 13.27 ug/h) uses a different preparation method and is not used (conflict noted) | [ASSUMPTION] (distribution form, within-engine CV and reference time); [TRANSFER: F404-400 CF188] (mean, 2 sigma) | Bennett et al. 1996 QFDA Tbl I (archive.org DTIC_ADP010173) via f404_in20_engine_model.md Q8; Schofield et al. ## W2.3a Engine and FADEC calibration, priority subset (M07, M08; AD-55 item 6)

| ID | Statement | Tag | Source / evidence | Sweep range | Affects | Exposing test | Replacement route | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A-W2.3-1 | M08's reference, accel/decel and feed-forward Wf/PS3 lines are derived (``phalanx.fadec.harmonise``) from M07's steady line of the **installed** nominal engine: SLS ISA with the aircraft's nominal ground extraction (``harm_bleed`` 0.503 kg/s, ``harm_pto`` 21.8 kW = M09's ground values on the W2.5 bench), and below ground idle the reference follows M07's installed steady line at the idle nozzle floor down to ``harm_line_min_n2c`` 0.55 (the steady Wf/PS3 rises as N2 falls there), with the W1.3b shape scaled to join below it (start regime). Replaces the no-load line, which left 3-20 % accel margin at idle under M09's loads and B7-level deterioration so a small sag rolled the engine back to WF_MIN (W2.5 DEVIATIONS D3) | [ASSUMPTION] | FADEC accel/decel schedules are laid out against the installed engine's steady line (C-MAPSS40k accel/decel limit structure, f404 notes Q4); M09 ground extraction measured on the W2.5 bench; F404 customer-bleed limits (TM-104247); IN20 schedules not public | harm_bleed 0-1.9 kg/s; harm_pto 0-60 kW; harm_line_min_n2c 0.50-0.62 | M08 ratio-unit tables (ru_ref, ru_ff), accel/decel limits, governor gain scale | `tests/integration/test_fadec_accel_margin.py`; `tests/integration/test_faults_bench_trim.py`; `tests/integration/test_fadec_harmonise.py` | IN20 FADEC schedules (GE/HAL) | open |
| A-W2.3-3 | M08's N2-governor small-signal PI gains are scheduled on corrected core speed: multiplier max(1, gov_ks_margin x (-dRU/dN2c) / (gov_kp x RU_ref)) along M07's installed steady line (``harmonise.governor_gain_schedule``, table ``gov_ks``), so 1 (AD-57's Kp 1.5, Ki 1.0) wherever the steady Wf/PS3 rises with N2 and above 1 near ground idle, where the installed engine's steady Wf/PS3 falls as N2 rises and constant-ratio-unit fuelling is barely damped. Margin 5 (x5.4 at ground idle on the W2.1b M07, up to x12.3 at N2c 0.63, 1 from N2c 0.69): the smallest tested margin whose W2.5 idle step (loads and each B7 deterioration stepped in from the no-load trim) overshoots below 23 % (damping ratio > 0.42) in every case.
